## business-model — module synthesis

_Source: `99_business-model-synthesis.md`_

# Business Model Reality Check — NU (Synthesis)

**Regime and basis, carried from triage `00`.** Nu Holdings Ltd. (NYSE: NU, Class A ordinary shares, USD) is a Cayman-incorporated **US SEC foreign private issuer** — the annual filing is **Form 20-F**, not a 10-K, and there is no DEF 14A (CLAUDE.md §27). Reporting standard **IFRS as issued by the IASB**; reporting currency **US dollars**, statements in thousands; fiscal year ends **31 December**. Auditor KPMG Auditores Independentes. All 13 upstream specialist outputs were read in full; none is missing.

**Three revenue bases appear in this module and they are NOT interchangeable.** (a) **IFRS total revenue** US$15,774.7m FY2025 `[FY25 Form 20-F, Statement of Income Data, p.155]`. (b) **Note-34 geographic base** US$12,083.8m — a narrower customer-facing subset, 76.6% of (a) `[FY25 Form 20-F, Note 34(b), p.F-97]`. (c) **Capital IQ bank-template "Total Revenue"** US$6,991.2m — revenue *after* interest expense and loan-loss provisions `[CIQ Financials → Income Statement export (NU) — vendor export]`. Every figure below names its base.

**Not used as evidence.** `data/NU/NU_Holdings_Deep_Dive_15_Page_Memo_30_Aug_2026.pdf` is a prior engine output carrying its own target and score. It is a §4 tier-9 user note; it did not inform any verdict, score or reconciliation here. Every upstream specialist independently excluded it too.

**Language.** Every filing in this pool is in English except a Portuguese translation of the FY2025 financial statements, the Q3'25 deck and four one-page Bradesco BDR depositary notices. Per CLAUDE.md §27 those are present at full source tier. No upstream module logged a language barrier, and no conviction cap here rests on one.

---

## Abstract

Nu Holdings is a branchless digital bank serving 139 million consumers in Brazil, Mexico and Colombia through one mobile app. It is a lender first: 85.2% of FY2025 revenue was interest income, and Brazil supplied 91.4% of the revenue the filing splits by country. The strongest positive is a measured cost advantage — 14,314 customers per employee against incumbents' 1,234 — producing a 31.6% return on equity against the 16.51% Brazilian cost of equity the company itself discloses. The strongest negative is that the whole profitable record sits inside one benign credit cycle, with the five-year loss-inclusive average return of 12.40% sitting below that same cost of equity. No disqualifier triggered; this reads as a cyclical business needing a timing edge.

---

### Automatic Disqualifier Check

Restated from `01_disqualifier-scan.md`. Denominators pinned there, all FY2025 from the same statement: total revenue US$15,774.7m, total expenses US$12,032.7m (gross basis), net income US$2,871.7m, control-group holding 906,212,615 shares (74.4% of voting power).

"The Group had no single customer that represented 10% or more of the Group's revenues" in FY2023, FY2024, FY2025 and H1'26 — `FY25 Form 20-F, Note 34(b)`; `Q2 FY26 Interim Report (14 Aug 2026), Note 34(b)` |

Filed operating cash flow +US$755.6m / +US$1,266.2m / +US$2,399.0m / +US$3,500.5m FY2022–FY2025 — `FY25 Form 20-F, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.F-14`; `FY23 Form 20-F (filed 19 Apr 2024)`. The vendor's `ltm_ocf_m` −10,304.8 is a **classification difference** (CIQ's Bank template moves the +US$12,861.3m deposit inflow from operating to financing), not a distress signal |

**No row is Y. No verdict lock is applied.** Near-miss test: 0 of 5 quantitative rows sat in the near-miss band, so **`RF-DISQ-001` was NOT emitted** and there is no sub-threshold mosaic to carry.

### Verdict

- **Verdict: Cyclical business — worth deeper work only with a strong timing edge**
- **Disqualifier triggered:** **N** — none of the eight.
- **Business clarity /100: 78.** The business is easy to describe and the company describes it consistently: one reportable segment, one dominant country, one dominant product. Held below 80 because the clarity is uneven where it matters most — no segment profit disclosure of any kind, 23.4% of FY2025 income given no country split, and every headline operating metric published on a company-defined non-IFRS managerial basis that does not tie to the audited statements `[03_segment-map §3; 05_customer-geography header; 02_business-identity §3a]`.
- **Business quality /100: 46** *(from `07_business-quality.md` — Mixed / Average band 41–60)*.
- **Moat /100: 78** — the strongest single moat source, **cost advantage**, per `09_moat.md §2`. **The overall moat verdict is "Narrow moat, trajectory stable", not "strong"** — carry that label with the number, never the number alone.
- **External dependency risk /100 *(INVERTED — higher = worse)*: 57** *(from `10_external-dependency.md §4)* — "Partly externally driven", band 41–60.
- **Capital allocation & governance /100: 50 — CAPPED** *(evidence-based score from `11_capital-allocation-governance.md §4` was **76**; see the capital-structure cap below).*
- **Data quality /100: 82.** Triage returned a **Sufficient** verdict with zero extraction failures across 115 sources, an audited FY2025 20-F five months old and three qualifying sources inside six months. Scored 82 rather than higher for the gaps triage and downstream named: heavy duplication (`Filings 2/` mirrors `Filings/`; five "(1)" workbook twins; seven CIQ conflicts logged), no peer filing in the pool at all, no EBITDA line on the bank template, no segment profit, no cohort-level cost, and no individual executive pay. *This number is my roll-up of triage's verdict and named gaps; triage itself published no numeric score.*
- **Overall usefulness /100: 63.**
- **Business type (one line):** Digital-only consumer bank in Latin America — an unsecured, credit-card-led lender funded by low-cost retail deposits, monetised by selling more products to an already-acquired customer base `[02_business-identity §3]`.
- **Biggest business-model risk (one line):** A Brazilian consumer credit downturn hitting a book that is 92% unsecured (card 66%, unsecured 26%, secured 8%) and grew 37% year on year FX-neutral, when the entire profitable record was earned with Brazilian unemployment *falling* from 6.2% to 5.1% and contains no downturn at anything like the current book size `[07_business-quality §4; 09_moat §3; Q2'26 deck, p.13; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 5 macroeconomic indicators table]`.

---

**REJECTOR-FILTER CAPS (CLAUDE.md §24).** This module owns three of the six filters. All three were tested; one tripped.

- **Filter 1 — Crooks / integrity: NOT tripped, and nothing soft to route.** `01_disqualifier-scan §1 row 6` searched specifically and found no proven fraud or defrauding of customers, suppliers, employees or shareholders by the controller or senior management, **and no short-seller report or unproven adverse allegation either** — so there is neither a lock nor a conviction-capping "buzz" item to carry. No cap applied on this filter.
- **Filter 4 — Serial acquirers: NOT tripped. No cap applied.** The acquisition-pattern row scored severity **15**, far below the ≥70 trip threshold: five years of cash acquisitions total US$131.9m (US$114.5m / US$10.3m / nil / US$5.6m / US$1.5m), goodwill flat at US$409.4m, no debt-funded deal, no deal near the company's own value `[11 §1 and §Acquisition-pattern test]`. `12_red-flags-sweep` **corrected the evidence base without changing the conclusion**: the US$153.0m Tyme Group commitment (US$102.4m in associates plus call options and warrants) sits outside the vendor "Cash Acquisitions" line, taking the true five-year figure to roughly US$285m — still about 4% of one year's revenue on the vendor base and 1.2% of equity, so Filter 4 still does not trip and the 50/70 caps do not bind `[12 §2, severity 42]`. Carry the correction, not a cap.
- **Filter 5 — Fast-changing industry: TRIPPED. Cap applied (not binding on the number).** `07_business-quality §1` scored the industry rate-of-change / disruption row **38 (≤40)** and emitted the tag. Under `MODULE_RULES.md` this caps Business quality at **65**; the evidence-based aggregate of 46 already sits below it, so the cap does not bind — but the **flag does**. Per `07 §4` and `09_moat §5`, **this thesis is a sector / technology-cycle bet on Brazilian digital financial services, not a durable-compounder holding**: the payment rails (Pix, 2020), the data rules (open finance), the price ceiling on card interest (Law 14,690/2023), the capital regime (Joint Res. 14; BCB Res. 477/478) and the virtual-asset regime (BCB Res. 519/520/521) have each been rewritten inside six years, and Nu's own rise from nothing to 118m Brazilian customers is the proof the competitive set reorders that fast. The same trip **discounts moat durability** and is the stated reason a return 1,000–1,500bp above the cost of equity is classified Narrow rather than Strong `[09_moat §5]`.

**RF-BQ-005 (fast-changing industry: rate-of-change ≤40)**

**CAPITAL STRUCTURE TRANSACTION CAP — APPLIED.**

**Capital allocation score capped at 50/100 due to the FY2025 step-up in borrowings, which raised total debt (excluding customer deposits) from US$2,097.5m at 31 Dec 2024 to US$5,277.2m at 31 Dec 2025 — a 151.6% year-on-year increase, and US$5,896.7m by 30 Jun 2026 — clearing the >50% total-debt-change trigger by a wide margin** `[11_capital-allocation-governance §1, debt row, citing CIQ Financials → Capital Structure Summary export, FY2024 / FY2025 / 3 months Jun-30-2026 — vendor export]`. The evidence-based score before the cap was 76/100. I am applying the cap rather than waiving it, for three reasons stated plainly: the trigger is defined numerically and is met by a factor of three; the same period also carried a new US$1.0bn buyback authorisation with US$500.4m already spent; and it lands while Tier 1 capital is falling 16.2% → 14.4% → 13.4% and non-performing loans are rising 6.7% → 7.8% → 7.9% → 8.6%, which is the exact combination `11 §3` names as the one that would move the classification from *owner-operator discipline* to *capital allocation concerns*. Clean hygiene — six unqualified KPMG opinions, no restatement, US$0.9m of related-party balances, zero intra-group trading rows — cannot override that, and that is what the cap exists to enforce.

**What the cap does NOT say, so the qualifier is not lost downstream:** this is **not** a distress finding, and `11` did not report a restructuring transaction. Borrowings excluding deposits are 9.5% of interest-bearing funding (deposits US$41,925.1m against borrowings US$4,398.2m at FY2025), debt-to-equity is 0.44×, equity grew from US$7.6bn to US$13.3bn in eighteen months, and the DFC facility — the only named outside credit line — is US$100m committed and fully undrawn after Nu prepaid the drawn US$50m `[06_value-chain §2; 11 §1]`. The cap is a mechanical, conservative response to a large percentage move in a small funding line, and the master synthesizer should carry both the capped 50 and the underlying 76 rather than either alone.

**FORENSIC TAG PROPAGATION (CLAUDE.md §13; eval check AQ).**

- **`RF-DISQ-001` — NOT emitted.** `01_disqualifier-scan §4` ran the near-miss test on all five quantitative rows and found 0 in band; every test clears its threshold by a wide margin rather than sitting just under it.
- **`RF-RFS-001 (aggressive accounting practice pattern)` — EMITTED and propagated.** Qualifying row from `12_red-flags-sweep §3`: internally developed software capitalisation, severity 52 — additions grew 84.8% (US$154,583k → US$285,670k) against 37.0% revenue growth, US$223,167k of cost stayed off the FY2025 income statement (5.8% of pre-tax profit, up from 4.2%), and US$55,708k — 19.5% of the year's additions — was disposed of in the same year. Also repeated as a bullet in Section 4.

**Module Disconfirmation (CLAUDE.md §8; fix F37).** Two-sided test the verdict above must survive.

- **Strongest bear point.** Three independent modules ring the current earnings level as a peak, each for a different reason, and they compound: `07 §4` (30%+ ROE and 12.4% risk-adjusted NIM earned with unemployment at 5.1%, the best of six quarters), `04 §3` (Q2'26 contribution of US$6.17 per active customer per month against a defensible FY2025 annual figure of US$4.88), and `12 §3` (deferred tax benefit of US$1,816,376k over FY2023–FY2025 = **30.9% of the entire US$5,874,284k profitable record**, while current tax actually payable *fell* 7.3% as pre-tax profit *rose* 38.4%). The tax argument is the sharpest because, unlike the credit argument, it does not need a downturn to bite — Brazilian rules cap deferred-tax-asset use at 30% of taxable profit a year and Complementary Law 224/2025 cut federal incentives 10% from January 2026.
- **Strongest bull point (the steelman).** The cost advantage is measured on both sides of the spread and is the highest-scoring moat row in the module at 78/100: cost to serve and G&A per active customer roughly **85% below** Brazilian incumbents', **14,314 customers per employee against an incumbent average of 1,234** (11.6×), against incumbents each running 1,685–3,955 branches and 49,661–85,206 employees; a 64-versus-43 NPS that delivers **71% of new Brazilian customers organically** at a US$7.4 cost to acquire; and deposits bought at **88% of the local interbank rate, 3 percentage points cheaper than a year earlier** `[09_moat §2; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.B and Item 5, p.157; Q2'26 deck, p.14]`. It converts into peer-leading returns on the matched vendor basis: net income margin 42.73% against a peer median of 27.37%, return on tangible book 29.2% against Itaú 24.3%, BTG 23.7%, Inter 16.7%, Bradesco 15.2%, Santander Brasil 14.7%, Banco do Brasil 9.6% — highest of all eleven `[08_competitive-map §2]`.
- **Single killer risk.** A Brazilian consumer credit downturn arriving while the book is 92% unsecured, 37%-a-year unseasoned, Tier 1 is sliding (16.2% → 14.4% → 13.4%) and US$28.8bn of unused card limits sits off balance sheet, up 63% in one year — the exposure that draws down hardest exactly when customers get stressed `[07 §1; 11 §1 and §3]`.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible, against the bear case.** Coverage is the one asset-quality series that has *not* deteriorated: total allowance ran 239% → 244% of the 90+ NPL stock across eleven quarters to Q2'26, and the Q2'26 allowance build was **113% of new 15+ day delinquency formation** — so the rising NPL ratio reads as book mix and seasoning of a fast-growing portfolio rather than under-provisioning, and the overlay's "rising NPAs with falling coverage" flag explicitly does **not** trip `[07 §1, cyclicality row; Q2'26 deck, p.19]`. Second: the company demonstrated measured mitigation, not narration — after the FGTS rule change cut that product's originations 50–60%, the loss "was more than offset by the growth in public consignado", and management attributed **two thirds** of the Q2'26 risk-adjusted-margin beat to its own credit performance against one third to the Desenrola policy programme `[10 §3]`.

Every verdict line below is quoted or paraphrased from the specialist's own words, with its qualifiers intact. Claim-fidelity pass applied against all four failure shapes (qualifier dropped, basis dropped, build dropped, verdict hardened).

Critical missing items: none. | The pool is smaller than 115 sources suggests — `Filings 2/` mirrors all 13 files in `Filings/`, five CIQ workbooks have "(1)" twins, and the 13-tab `Financials.xls` overlaps eleven single-sheet exports (7 conflicts logged). **Never count a duplicate as corroboration.** Also: CIQ uses a **Bank template**, so no EBITDA row exists and EV/EBITDA, net-debt/EBITDA and interest coverage are unavailable — do not manufacture them. |
| `01_disqualifier-scan` | **No disqualifier triggered (0 of 8). No verdict lock.** Near-misses in band: 0 of 5; no compounding signal; `RF-DISQ-001` not emitted. | The vendor's negative LTM operating cash flow (−US$10,304.8m) versus the filing's positive +US$3,500.5m is a **classification difference, not a misread**: CIQ's Bank template moves the +US$12,861.3m deposit inflow out of operating into financing. Same number, two shelves; the audited filing governs. |
| `02_business-identity` | **Digital-only consumer bank in Latin America** — an unsecured, credit-card-led lender funded by low-cost retail deposits. All nine bank/lender overlay KPIs present; no required KPI absent. | The marketing layer stripped: the filing calls Nu "a technology company that is revolutionizing a broad range of services", but the audited income statement shows **85% of revenue as interest** (US$13,434.7m of US$15,774.8m). The free app and free transfers are how it gets the customer, not how it gets paid. |
| `03_segment-map` | **One operating and reportable segment (Banking, the whole Group), 100% of revenue and 100% of profit** — the company's own IFRS 8 position, unchanged word-for-word across FY2023–FY2025 and H1'26. | **There is no segment profit disclosure of any kind** — no country margin, no product margin, no country net income. And Note 34's geographic base excludes US$3,690,975k of treasury income, **23.4% of FY2025 group interest and fee income**, so the geographic split is a split of customer-facing revenue, not of earnings. |
| `04_unit-economics` | **Creates value — clearly, and by a wide margin on the disclosed numbers — but at Q2'26 returns that are the highest in the disclosed series, so the level should not be treated as structural.** | Per active customer per month, Q2'26 [IFRS]: revenue 16.05 − funding 4.53 − transactional 0.37 − ECL 4.32 = gross profit 6.83 − cost to serve 0.66 = **contribution 6.17** (~US$74/year) against a one-off US$7.4 cost to acquire. **Ring-fenced as a peak quarter:** risk-adjusted NIM of 12.42% was the highest of six quarters (range 9.30–12.42%, six-quarter average 10.40%) and was 9.48% one quarter earlier; the **defensible through-cycle figure is nearer the FY2025 annual US$4.88**, not US$6.17. |
| `05_customer-geography` | **Geography-concentrated with essentially zero customer concentration** — and the concentration is **naked, not contractually secured**. | Brazil is **91.4%** of the Note-34 geographic base (90.9% in H1'26), and **no lower than 70.0% of total revenue** even assuming none of the unallocated US$3,690.9m treasury book is Brazilian. No customer has reached 10% of revenue in any of the last four reported periods. No long-term customer contract, no minimum term, no switching penalty is disclosed anywhere. |
| `06_value-chain` | **Mixed** — strong upstream, constrained downstream. Nu manufactures its own funding; it does not set the price of a fifth of what it earns. | **On pass-through, carry both halves:** there is **no *contractual* escalator on Nu's revenue** — a fact about contracts — but the **measured recovery on the deposit-price lever alone is roughly 40%** of the US$473.2m FY2025 rate shock (3pp of benchmark reclaimed ≈ US$189m), and the spread measures had more than recovered by Q2'26. In the FY2025 annual accounts the gross margin still fell 361bp. Biggest bargaining risk: **Mastercard**, the named sole source behind US$1,720.3m of card income and a US$26bn card book. |
| `07_business-quality` | **46 / 100 — Mixed / Average.** "Not a durable compounder on the evidence available; a high-quality operator inside a cyclical, regulator-shaped industry." | **Filter 5 trips at rate-of-change 38 (`RF-BQ-005`).** Weakest row is regulatory dependence at **20/100**: the price of 38% of revenue capped by statute, the fee on another 14.2% set by Mastercard and capped at 0.7%, funding at 88% of the interbank rate, one rule change cutting a product's originations 50–60%, and a US$186.4m FGC levy for other banks' failures. Strongest row is customer stickiness at 74 — 0.1% monthly net churn, 83.5% activity, primary relationship for over 62% of active customers. |
| `08_competitive-map` | **Gaining share — but the rate of gain has slowed sharply, and that qualifier travels with the verdict.** Market shape: a top-heavy market with several similarly sized large players, not one dominant firm. | Brazilian credit-card purchase-volume share (ABECS): 8.7% (2021) → 11.6% → 13.9% → 14.2% → 14.7% (2025). **Read the increments: +2.9pp, +2.3pp, then +0.3pp and +0.5pp** — the pace in the last two years is roughly a fifth of the first two. The number cutting the other way, named: management's own estimate is only **"7% market share of that profit pool"**, and the 20-F puts group share at ~5% of SAM — a large share of the transactions, a small share of the profits. |
| `09_moat` | **Narrow moat. Trajectory: stable.** Neither "widening confirmed" nor "erosion confirmed" may be carried downstream. | The economic test, with its full build: **31.6% ROE (LTM to 30 Jun 2026) against a 16.51% company-disclosed Brazilian cost of equity (+1,512bp), or +1,063bp against an independent 21% CAPM estimate** — *but* the **five-year loss-inclusive average ROE of 12.40% sits BELOW that same 16.51% (−411bp)**, and the profitable-era average of 27.06% overstates the through-cycle level because the whole profitable record sits inside a benign Brazilian consumer environment. **A true through-cycle ROE cannot be computed from this pool.** Strongest moat source: cost advantage 78/100. |
| `10_external-dependency` | **Partly externally driven.** External dependency risk **57 / 100 — INVERTED, higher = worse** (band 41–60: material exposure, mixed mitigation). | Biggest single lever is **the Brazilian real against the US dollar**: the company's own 17.8% BRL shock moves the Brazilian net investment US$1,369.8m, and all subsidiaries US$1,509.6m — **13.3% of total equity** — on translation exposure management explicitly "decided not to hedge". Larger than the 400bp Brazilian rate shock (US$306.3m) and the full downside credit scenario (US$381.5m) combined. |
| `11_capital-allocation-governance` | **Owner-operator discipline**, with two named structural caveats. Score **76 / 100** before this synthesis applied the capital-structure cap. | The signal to watch is **asset quality against a capital cushion the buyback is drawing down**: NPLs 6.7% → 7.8% → 7.9% → 8.6% while Tier 1 fell 16.2% → 14.4% → 13.4%, with US$500.4m of a US$1.0bn buyback already spent and US$28.8bn of unused off-balance-sheet card limits up 63% in a year. **Filter 4 explicitly does NOT trip** (acquisition severity 15). Caveats: NYSE "controlled company" status with 74.4% of votes held by one man, and no individual executive pay disclosed (a Cayman legal-regime gap, not opacity). |
| `12_red-flags-sweep` | **Eight new flags, all at severity 42–58.** Most severe: reliance on non-cash deferred-tax credits (58). **`RF-RFS-001 (aggressive accounting practice pattern)` emitted** on the software-capitalisation row (52). | **Deferred tax benefit of US$1,816,376k over FY2023–FY2025 = 30.9% of the entire US$5,874,284k profitable record**, while current tax actually payable FELL 7.3% (US$1,536,521k → US$1,424,006k) as pre-tax profit ROSE 38.4% (US$2,795,198k → US$3,868,419k). Carry the qualifier exactly: this is fully disclosed accounting, correctly applied — the flag is about the **durability** of the reported profit, not about the accounting being wrong. |

## 3. Reconciliation

Eight material disagreements or basis conflicts were found between specialists. Each is resolved below on the conservative default (CLAUDE.md §4) with the reconciled view named.

**1. Loan-to-deposit ratio — three figures for the same quarter (`06` vs `07`, flagged by `12`).**
`06_value-chain §1 and §4` used the CFO's **35%** ("lends out only 35 cents of every dollar it holds"); `07_business-quality §1` used the deck's **average LDR 58%** (Q2'26, up from 50% in Q4'25) and the vendor's gross-loans-to-deposits of **88.9%** (LTM Jun-2026, from 76.8% FY2024 and 80.3% FY2025). `12 §2` (severity 50) established why they differ: the deck's 58% has a published definition (net credit portfolio ÷ available funding less compulsory deposits, plus financial bills) and the vendor's 88.9% is gross loans ÷ total deposits on the audited balance sheet, while **the 35% appears nowhere in the deck, carries no definition and no reconciliation in any document in this pool**.
**Reconciled view carried forward:** use the two *defined* measures — **58% (managerial, company-defined)** and **88.9% (vendor, audited balance sheet)** — and state which. **Do not carry the 35% as if it were the same measure.** Both `06` and `07` were quoting the company faithfully; the defect is the company's, not either specialist's. Conservative reading: the balance sheet is being pushed harder, not held at 35%.

**2. Operating FX — "matched" versus a 26.9% dollar-linked cost base (`10` vs `12`).**
`10_external-dependency §4` listed "Operating FX matched — no significant unhedged FX exposures" as a **mitigating** driver of the 57 score, concluding "cash flows are insulated even though reported USD figures are not". `12 §2` (severity 45) showed that the Item 11 statement covers **financial-instrument** exposures only, while a separate risk factor states "**around 26.9% of our costs in the year ended December 31, 2025 were directly or indirectly linked to the U.S. dollar**, whereas the majority of our revenue was denominated in *reais*".
**Reconciled view:** the two statements are not strictly contradictory but **they cannot both be summarised as "matched"**. The conservative reading is adopted: financial-instrument exposure is matched; **the operating cost base is not**. Sized as a **zero-mitigation bound (§9), not a forecast**: 26.9% of FY2025 total expenses of US$12,032,723k ≈ US$3,236.8m of dollar-linked cost, so a 10% BRL depreciation adds roughly **US$324m** of cost in local-currency terms, about 11.3% of FY2025 net income, before any repricing or re-sourcing. The pool discloses no realised offset, so none can be computed.

**3. Five-year acquisition spend and the Filter-4 evidence base (`11` vs `12`).**
`11 §1` tested Filter 4 against the vendor "Cash Acquisitions" line: US$131.9m across FY2021–FY2025, severity 15, filter does not trip. `12 §2` (severity 42) found the **US$153.0m Tyme Group commitment** (18.0% stake, US$102.4m in associates plus call options and warrants, no voting rights, US$3,689k loss in associates in FY2025) sitting outside that line.
**Reconciled view:** the corrected five-year figure is roughly **US$285m**, about 4% of FY2025 revenue on the vendor base and 1.2% of equity. **Filter 4 still does not trip and no cap follows** — `12` said so itself. What is carried forward is the *character* of the spend: capital placed in emerging markets Nu does not operate in, with no votes, plus options to put in more.

**4. Cost to serve — annual falling, quarterly rising (`04` internal, adjudicated there).**
Annual: US$0.9 (FY2024) → US$0.8 (FY2025), improving. Quarterly: US$0.8 (Q2'25) → US$1.0 (Q2'26), +14% FX-neutral, deteriorating.
**Reconciled view (as `04 §2` adjudicated by name):** the quarterly series is more recent, more granular, and confirmed by the IFRS lines underneath it — customer support and operations rose 40.1% year on year in Q2'26 (US$226,246k vs US$161,434k) against a 13.8% rise in average active customers. **Current direction is deteriorating.** It has not yet mattered: cost to serve is US$1.0 of US$16.05 revenue per unit and contribution per unit still rose 34%.

**5. Card take rate — direction unsettled inside one MD&A paragraph (`06`, carried by `09`).**
Card income rose 24.6% on purchase-volume growth of **17%**, implying the take rate **rose ~6.5%**. The same paragraph also states "total purchase volume grew **29%**", on which the take rate **fell 3.4%**.
**Reconciled view:** the 17% is the matched basis (the filing attaches it directly to the card-income line) and is used as primary — **but the direction of this one measure is not settled by the pool and must never be quoted without the 29% alongside it.** `06` and `09` both carried it correctly; the qualifier travels.

**6. Which revenue base a margin is computed on (`03`, `08`, `09`).**
Nu's net margin reads **42.73%** on the CIQ post-provision bank template, **23.8%** on the 20-F's own Note-34 base, and **18.2%** on IFRS total revenue.
**Reconciled view:** all three are correct on their own basis and none is interchangeable. **The peer comparison is valid only on the CIQ basis**, because all eleven companies in the comps workbook sit on the same template — and it is therefore not comparable to any company's own filed revenue line. Any claim that Nu "earns more than peers" must state that basis or it is not testable.

**7. Brazil's share of the company — 91.4% or 70.0% (`03` vs `05`).**
`03` reports Brazil at 91.4% of the Note-34 base; `05` shows that base is only 76.6% of total revenue, so on total revenue Brazil is **at most 70.0%** if none of the unallocated US$3,690.9m treasury income is Brazilian, and higher to the extent it is.
**Reconciled view:** not a disagreement — the same fact stated at two scopes. **Carry "91.4% of geographically-attributed revenue, and no lower than 70.0% of total revenue"** as one phrase. Both trip the >50%-one-geography concentration flag either way.

**8. Vendor figures that do not tie to the filing (`03`, `08`).**
Three cases, all resolved toward the filing or the tying source: CIQ's FY2024 geographic revenue (Brazil 9,478.5 / total 10,330.2) does not tie to the 20-F's Brazil 8,409,961 / total 9,051,257 — the gap of 1,278,980 is exactly the FY2024 treasury line the vendor mis-slotted, so **use the filing**; CIQ's Competitors export puts Inter & Co at US$158.6m against the comps workbook's US$1,279.1m for the same date — **use US$1,279.1m**; and the Competitors export carries LTM dates as old as 31 Dec 2022 (PicPay) and 31 Dec 2023 (Mercado Pago) — **do not treat those as current**.

---

## 4. Note To The Final Synthesizer

**MANDATORY RED-FLAG PROPAGATION — all eight new flags from `12_red-flags-sweep` score ≥40 and all eight appear below. None is omitted.**

- **Strongest business-model positive — a cost advantage measured on both sides of the spread, not asserted.** Cost to serve and G&A per active customer roughly **85% below** Brazilian incumbents' (the company's own estimate off incumbents' published FY2025 accounts); **14,314 customers per employee against an incumbent average of 1,234** (11.6×), while incumbents each run 1,685–3,955 branches and 49,661–85,206 employees; deposits bought at **88% of the local interbank rate, 3pp cheaper than a year earlier**, all US$41.9bn "acquired directly, without the use of third-party brokers or intermediaries" `[09_moat §2; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.B and Item 5; Q2'26 deck, p.14]`. What this MEANS: the advantage is a *cost* advantage, not pricing power — Nu can lower what it pays; it largely cannot raise what it charges.
- **Strongest business-model negative — the entire profitable record was earned in one favourable window, and three separate modules say so independently.** Credit: 30%+ ROE and a 12.4% risk-adjusted NIM (best of six quarters) earned with Brazilian unemployment falling 6.2% → 5.1%, on a book 92% unsecured `[07 §4]`. Unit economics: Q2'26 contribution of US$6.17 per active customer per month is a **peak-quarter reading**; the defensible annual figure is US$4.88 `[04 §3]`. Tax: **deferred tax credits are 30.9% of the whole US$5,874,284k profitable record**, and current tax payable *fell* 7.3% while pre-tax profit *rose* 38.4% `[12 §2, severity 58]`. What this MEANS: the reported earnings level is not a run rate, and the tax leg does not need a downturn to bite.
- **Most important segment.** There is only one — Banking, the whole Group, 100% of revenue and 100% of profit, the company's own IFRS 8 position. Inside it, **Brazil is the segment that matters**: 91.4% of geographically-attributed revenue, 90.2% of the regulatory capital in the regulated operating entities, and 80.4% of deposits. Mexico is growing fastest (5.8% → 6.7% → 7.2% of the base over eighteen months, +87% in H1'26) but is still far from balancing Brazil. **Profit by country is not disclosed at all** — do not construct it from revenue shares.
- **Cleanest unit-economics read.** Per active customer per month, Q2'26 on the audited-standard IFRS lines: revenue 16.05 − funding 4.53 − transactional 0.37 − expected credit loss 4.32 = **gross profit 6.83** − cost to serve 0.66 = **contribution 6.17** (≈US$74 a year) against a **one-off US$7.4 cost to acquire** — about a 1.5-month payback on the blended base, with net churn of 0.1% a month. **The honest version is longer:** a *new* customer is not an average one — the cohort curve starts at US$1.3 of monthly revenue and does not reach the group average until roughly month 48, putting a first-year cohort's payback nearer months seven to nine. **What cannot be derived, and it is the single most valuable missing disclosure: cohort-level contribution margin or cost of credit.** Revenue by cohort is published; cost by cohort is published nowhere, so a genuinely new customer's payback can only be inferred by borrowing the mature book's margin — the assumption most likely to be wrong in the optimistic direction. The company **withdrew** the LTV/CAC ratio (">30x") and the "payback in under 12 months" claim from the FY2025 20-F with no replacement.
- **Where the company sits versus named peers on margin and returns.** Top of its named peer set on both, all on the identical CIQ bank template as of 29 Aug 2026: net income margin **42.73%** against Itaú 32.58%, Bradesco 26.07%, Inter & Co 22.92% and a peer median of 27.37%; return on tangible book **29.2%** against Itaú 24.3%, BTG 23.7%, Inter 16.7%, Bradesco 15.2%, Santander Brasil 14.7%, Banco do Brasil 9.6% — highest of eleven. **Three qualifiers travel with that:** no ROIC/WACC comparison exists because the bank template has no EBITDA, EBIT or gross-margin row; **no peer filing is in the pool at all**, so every peer number is a tier-5 vendor export or a dated web read of a peer's own release; and Nu's superiority is three and a half years old inside one benign credit environment, against Itaú's twenty-plus-year record of mid-twenties returns.
- **Main external dependency — the Brazilian real, not the Brazilian interest rate.** The company's own 17.8% BRL shock moves the Brazilian net investment **US$1,369.8m** and all subsidiaries **US$1,509.6m — 13.3% of total equity** — on translation exposure it has **explicitly chosen not to hedge**. That is larger than the 400bp Brazilian rate shock (US$306.3m) and the full downside credit scenario (US$381.5m) combined. What this MEANS: the underlying Brazilian business would be untouched in local currency — this is damage to the reported USD figures and to the value of the NYSE line a dollar investor actually holds, not to the operations. Second-order and separate: `12` (severity 45) sized the *operating* mismatch — ~26.9% of costs dollar-linked against mostly-real revenue, a ~US$324m bound on a 10% BRL move.
- **Most important capital allocation and governance signal — asset quality set against a capital cushion that is being spent down.** NPLs rose every year (6.7% → 7.8% → 7.9% → 8.6% of loans through LTM Jun-2026) while Tier 1 fell 16.2% → 14.4% → 13.4%, and the company began buying back stock in the middle of that trend (US$500.4m of a US$1.0bn authorisation spent by 30 Jun 2026, at an average US$12.31 against a US$14.30 close on 29 Aug 2026). Alongside sits **US$28.8bn of unused off-balance-sheet card limits, up 63% in a year** `[12, severity 30 in 11's table]`. What this MEANS: today none of it is alarming — ROE 30.3%, equity up from US$7.6bn to US$13.3bn in eighteen months, allowance US$6,612.9m, buyback a quarter of one half-year's profit — but if the cycle turns, the same repurchase stops being surplus cash returned and becomes capital taken out of a thinning cushion. **Falsification test, dated and cheap: Q3 2026 results, due November 2026.**
- **The governance fact nobody upstream of `12` had:** the founder holds a **contractual veto** — under the Shareholder's Agreement (29 Nov 2021, last amended 11 Mar 2025) — over declaring any dividend, any merger or change of control, any share issuance, asset moves above 20% of net equity, material changes to strategic direction, related-party transactions and **the annual compensation of any officer or director**, for so long as he holds **at least 10% of voting power** — which on the filing's own share counts is roughly **121.4m Class B shares, about 2.5% of shares in issue**. He could sell down to a fortieth of the company and still veto every dividend. Separate 5% rights cover pro-rata designation of Audit and Risk Committee members `[12, severity 56]`. This is a contract, not a voting outcome; minority holders cannot vote it away, and it is what makes `11`'s "no dividend policy" row harder to solve.
- **The remaining flags at severity ≥40, each named so none is silently dropped:** software capitalisation running at 2.3× the rate of the business with 19.5% of the year's additions written off in the same year (**52** — the `RF-RFS-001` qualifying row); three published loan-to-deposit ratios for one quarter, the most reassuring of which has no definition anywhere (**50**); a **US$186.4m FGC levy for other banks' failures carried as an asset**, with neither the total multi-year obligation nor any recovery basis disclosed (**48**); the 26.9% dollar-linked cost base (**45**); the 20-F naming **one man** as the key-personnel risk while he holds founder, chairman and CEO simultaneously, with **no succession plan disclosed anywhere in the pool** (**44**); and the US$153.0m Tyme Group stake outside the acquisition line (**42**). `12`'s cross-cutting read is worth carrying whole: cost is being moved off the income statement in three places at once (software, deferred card-issuance costs, the FGC levy) in a year the reported profit also carried a US$427,259k deferred tax credit — **a reader rebuilding FY2025 profit on a cash-cost basis would land materially below US$2,871,672k**.
- **Whether any automatic disqualifier triggered.** **No** — none of the eight, and 0 of 5 quantitative near-misses, so no sub-threshold mosaic. `RF-DISQ-001` not emitted. **`RF-RFS-001 (aggressive accounting practice pattern)` IS emitted and is propagated here** for the cross-module forensic roll-up. So is **`RF-BQ-005 (fast-changing industry: rate-of-change ≤40)`**.
- **Which rejector filters tripped, and the cap each applied.** Filter 1 (crooks/integrity): **not tripped**, and no unverified adverse buzz exists to route — no cap. Filter 4 (serial acquirers): **not tripped** at severity 15, corrected evidence base ~US$285m over five years — no cap. Filter 5 (fast-changing industry): **TRIPPED** at rate-of-change 38 — caps business quality at 65 (not binding at 46), **flags the thesis as a sector / technology-cycle bet rather than a durable compounder**, and discounts moat durability. Separately, the **capital-structure transaction cap is APPLIED**, taking capital allocation and governance from an evidence-based 76 to **50**, on total debt rising 151.6% year on year (US$2,097.5m → US$5,277.2m).
- **Biggest missing data point.** **Cohort-level contribution margin or cost of credit** — the one disclosure that would let a genuinely new customer's payback be computed rather than inferred, and the gap the company made worse by withdrawing LTV/CAC and the "under 12 months" payback claim from the FY2025 20-F. Two close seconds, both of which would change how this business is sized rather than how it is described: **any segment profit disclosure at all** (there is none — no country, no product), and **peer filings in the pool** (there are none, so every competitor number is tier-5 vendor or dated web). Note explicitly: **there is no language-related gap here.** Every material document is in English or is a translated filing read at full source tier; no data request should be spent on an English version of anything.
- **Whether the business deserves deeper work, and what would change the answer.** **Yes, but as a cyclical position needing a timing edge, not as a compounder to buy and hold.** The franchise is real and measured; the returns are peak-cycle on three independent readings. **What would move it toward "high-quality — worth deeper work":** Q3 2026 results (November 2026) showing risk-adjusted NIM holding above 12% with 90+ NPL flat or lower and Tier 1 stabilising, plus the FY2026 20-F (around April 2027) printing a card-share increment back above +1pp — that combination would move the moat trajectory from stable to widening and the level toward Strong. **What would move it toward "low-quality — avoid deeper work":** risk-adjusted NIM falling back toward its 10.4% six-quarter average while 90+ NPL climbs past 6.9% and Tier 1 slides below 13.4% with buybacks continuing; **coverage breaking** (it has held 239–252% of 90+ NPL for eleven quarters and is the series to watch fail first); the deferred-tax credit reversing as Complementary Law 224/2025 bites; or any evidence that the 35%-versus-88.9% loan-to-deposit gap conceals a balance sheet lent out harder than the headline implies.

---

## 5. Simple Summary

- **What it does.** A bank with no branches. 139 million people in Brazil, Mexico and Colombia get a credit card, a loan, a deposit account and some investments through one phone app.
- **How it makes money.** By lending, not by apps. **85% of FY2025 revenue was interest** — US$13,434.7m of US$15,774.8m — against 15% in fees. The free app and free transfers are how it gets the customer; credit is how it gets paid.
- **Does each new unit create value?** Yes, clearly. One active customer threw off **US$6.17 a month after every variable cost and the full cost to serve in Q2'26** — about US$74 a year — against a one-off **US$7.4** to acquire. But Q2'26 is the best quarter ever disclosed; **the honest through-cycle figure is nearer US$4.88 a month**, and the cost of serving a *new* customer's credit risk is not published at all.
- **Which segment matters most.** There is only one segment, and inside it only one country: **Brazil, 91.4% of the revenue the filing splits by geography** (no lower than 70% of total revenue). Mexico is growing fastest and is still nowhere near balancing it.
- **Does it have a moat, and against whom?** **A narrow one, and it is a cost moat.** Against Itaú, Bradesco, Santander Brasil, Banco do Brasil, Caixa, BTG, Inter & Co, C6, MercadoPago, PicPay, PagSeguro and StoneCo — all named by Nu itself. It serves **14,314 customers per employee against their 1,234**, and out-earns every listed one of them. It is narrow rather than strong because the whole profitable record is three and a half years old, all of it inside a good Brazilian credit cycle, and the **five-year average return of 12.40% is below the 16.51% cost of equity the company itself discloses**.
- **What external variables it depends on.** The Brazilian real (unhedged translation exposure worth 13.3% of equity on the company's own shock), the Brazilian central bank's rate (it pays 88% of the interbank rate for its deposits), Brazilian law (the price of 38% of revenue is capped by statute; interchange on another slice is capped at 0.7%), and the Brazilian consumer's ability to pay (the book is 92% unsecured).
- **Is capital allocated well?** Mostly yes on hygiene — no dividends but a 30% return on retained profit, near-zero acquisitions, six clean audit opinions, a share count that has just turned down, US$0.9m of related-party balances. **But the score is capped at 50 here** because borrowings jumped 151.6% in one year, and because bad loans are rising (6.7% → 8.6%) while the regulator's capital cushion is falling (16.2% → 13.4%) and the company is buying back stock. Also: one man can veto any dividend by contract, down to owning about 2.5% of the shares.
- **Does it deserve deeper work?** **Yes — but as a cyclical bet that needs timing, not as a business to own through anything.** The earnings are peak-cycle for three separate reasons (credit conditions, per-customer contribution, and non-cash tax credits worth 30.9% of the entire profitable record), and the industry it operates in has had its rules rewritten five times in six years.

### 1A. External Data

**None.** There is no `external/` subfolder in the frozen `DATA_PATH`, and no manifest row carries `external: true` or a `provenance` object. Nothing in this pool is externally sourced research, so no external document affects the sufficiency verdict.

### 1B. Business-Relationship Export

`GENERATION_ROOT/relationships.json` (`relationship-graph/v1`) is present and reports 2 sources: `Nu Holdings Ltd NYSE NU Customers.xls` (1 row) and `Nu Holdings Ltd NYSE NU Suppliers.xls` (10 rows).

- **11 disclosed relationship rows** across **12 named counterparties**.
- **9 are genuine outside parties** (`third_party`); 3 are the company's own group entities (`group`); **0 `likely_group`**, 0 intra-group rows (`intragroup_row_share_pct` = 0.0).
- **3 of the third parties are listed** (2 suppliers, 1 customer) across ENXTPA, LSE and NYSE — e.g. Edenred SE (ENXTPA:EDEN), Wise Group plc, MasterCard International Incorporated.
- **Scope, quoted verbatim:** "Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)"; "Include Customers for:Current subsidiaries"; "Recently disclosed suppliers only (within the last two years)"; "Include Suppliers for:Current subsidiaries."
- This is a §4 tier-5 vendor export, not a filing. It proves a relationship exists; it never sizes one. It is enrichment only — it does not move the sufficiency verdict, and its absence would not have been a gap.

## 2A. Filing Regime

| Item | Detected Value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Primary listing country | United States — NYSE, Class A ordinary shares. Incorporated in the Cayman Islands; principal executive contact and operations in São Paulo, Brazil. A secondary Brazilian **BDR Level I** programme trades on B3 with Banco Bradesco S.A. as depositary. | `FY25 Form 20-F, cover page` (Cayman Islands jurisdiction of incorporation; commission file 001-41129; Rua Capote Valente 39, Pinheiros – São Paulo); `data/NU/Filings/Nu_Holdings_Ltd_-_Form_Interim_Report(Aug-20-2026).pdf` (Bradesco BDR Nível I depositary notice) |
| Filing regime | **US SEC — foreign private issuer.** Primary documents are Form 20-F (annual) and Form 6-K-equivalent interim/press material; the Brazilian BDR notices are secondary, B3-facing. Do NOT mark 10-K / 10-Q / DEF 14A "missing" — the 20-F is the local equivalent and it is present. | `FY25 Form 20-F, cover page` ("ANNUAL REPORT PURSUANT TO SECTION 13 OR 15(d) OF THE SECURITIES EXCHANGE ACT OF 1934", box checked) |
| Reporting standard | **IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the IASB** (the 20-F cover ticks IFRS, not U.S. GAAP). Auditor: KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda. | `FY25 Form 20-F, cover page` ("International Financial Reporting Standards - Accounting Standards"); `Q2 FY26 Interim Report (14 Aug 2026), Independent Auditors' Report` (KPMG) |
| Reporting currency + fiscal-year end | **US dollars**, presented in thousands (millions in the CIQ exports). Fiscal year ends **31 December**. Note: functional currencies of the operating subsidiaries are local (BRL, MXN, COP) and translate into a USD reporting currency — any cross-currency claim must carry its FX date and rate (§15/§27). | `FY25 Form 20-F, cover page` ("For the fiscal year ended December 31, 2025"); `FY25 Annual Report (Portuguese translation, Feb-26-2026), Demonstrações do Resultado Consolidadas` ("Em milhares de dólares"); `ciq_facts.json` currency: USD |
| Document language(s) | **English** (primary: all 20-Fs, all interim financial statements, all transcripts, Q1'26 and Q2'26 releases and decks) and **Portuguese** (the FY2025 Annual Report translation dated 26 Feb 2026, the Q3'25 earnings deck dated 13 Nov 2025, and four Bradesco BDR depositary notices). Per CLAUDE.md §27 the Portuguese documents are PRESENT at full source tier — read and translate them; language is not a data gap and must not lower any score. | Extracts under `GENERATION_ROOT`; e.g. "Demonstrações financeiras consolidadas para o exercício findo em 31 de dezembro de 2025 e 2024" |

Downstream agents: apply CLAUDE.md §27 with the US-SEC foreign-private-issuer map. The interim reporting basis here is **both** — the 14 Aug 2026 interim report carries a standalone three-month AND a cumulative six-month column, so any consensus figure must be restated onto whichever basis is being tested before it is used as a bar (§27).

---

## 3. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** The pool holds an audited annual filing from within 18 months (Form 20-F for FY2025, filed 8 April 2026 — 5.0 months old) and three separate qualifying recent sources from within 6 months (interim financial statements for the six months ended 30 June 2026 filed 14 Aug 2026, the Q2 2026 earnings call transcript of 13 Aug 2026, and the Q2'26 investor deck of 13 Aug 2026), with zero extraction failures across all 115 sources.
- **Critical missing items:** None — the sufficiency rule is met on both legs.

**Caveats downstream agents must carry (none of these changes the verdict):**

1. **Heavy duplication.** The entire `Filings/` folder is mirrored as `Filings 2/` (13 identical files), five CIQ workbooks have "(1)" twin copies, the 13-tab `Financials.xls` overlaps eleven single-sheet exports (`ciq_facts.json` logs 7 conflicts), the two 20-F files exist as both `.pdf` and `.doc`, `consolidated_tax_report_2025-26.xlsx` duplicates 11 tabs of the IBKR workbook, and `Interim_Report(May-15-2026)` duplicates `(May-14-2026)`. The pool is smaller than 115 sources suggests. Never count a duplicate as corroboration (§16: "the methods agree" counts only if the sources are independent).
2. **No EBITDA line exists.** CIQ uses a Bank template, so `ltm_ebitda_m`, `net_debt_ebitda_x`, `interest_coverage_x`, `ev_ebitda_current_x` and `peer_ev_ebitda` are all `unknown` in `ciq_facts.json`. EV/EBITDA is not available for this name — do not manufacture it; use bank-appropriate methods.
3. **`net_debt_m` −9,274.2 is the CIQ vendor basis**, which may net short-term and liquid investments. It is not the strict total-debt-minus-cash figure. Label the basis every time it appears (§15) and prefer the 20-F's own numbers.
4. **`ltm_ocf_m` −10,304.8** is a lending balance-sheet growing, not cash burn. Do not read it as a distress signal without the 20-F cash-flow note.
5. **The 15-page deep-dive memo dated 30 Aug 2026 is a prior engine output**, not evidence. It already carries a target and a score. Treat it as a user note (§4 tier 9) and re-derive every conclusion from filings; do not let its verdict anchor this run.
6. **`Nu Holdings Ltd. Form 20-F filed on Apr-08-2026.pdf` (6,115 chars) is a search-index page**, not the filing. It extracted cleanly, but the substantive 20-F is the 1.67m-char PDF. Cite the full document.
7. **Four Portuguese Bradesco BDR notices are 340–360 chars each.** They extracted completely — they are one-page depositary notices, not failed extractions. They confirm the B3 BDR programme and the release dates; they carry no financial detail.
8. **Three files in the pool are not NU evidence at all:** an unrelated `.torrent` file, and the user's own Interactive Brokers activity statement and FY2025-26 tax workbooks. Exclude them from every company claim.

## business-model / 01_disqualifier-scan.md

_Source: `01_disqualifier-scan.md`_

# Disqualifier Scan — NU

**Filing regime (from triage `00`):** US SEC foreign private issuer. Annual filing = **Form 20-F**; interim = unaudited interim condensed consolidated financial statements furnished on Form 6-K. Reporting standard **IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the IASB**; reporting currency **US dollars**, statements in thousands; fiscal year ends **31 December**. Auditor: KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda. (PCAOB ID 1124). Do not mark 10-K / 10-Q / DEF 14A "missing" — the 20-F is the local equivalent and it is in the pool (CLAUDE.md §27). Two Portuguese documents in the pool (the Feb-2026 translated annual financial statements and the Bradesco BDR depositary notices) are disclosed sources, not gaps.

**Denominators pinned for this scan (all FY2025, from the same statement, same period, same currency):**

- **Total revenue = US$15,774,741 thousand (US$15,774.7m)** — `FY25 Form 20-F, Consolidated Statements of Income, p.F-7`.
- **Total expenses = US$12,032,723 thousand (US$12,032.7m)** — built as total cost of financial and transactional services US$9,149,739k *plus* gross operating expenses US$2,882,984k (customer support and operations 651,760 + general and administrative 1,420,460 + marketing 302,822 + other expenses 507,942), before other income of US$130,090k. Same source, same page. On the net basis the 20-F actually prints (operating expenses shown net of other income) the figure is US$11,902,633k; every ratio below is shown on the larger, more conservative gross base, and neither base changes any Y/N call.
- **Net income = US$2,871,672 thousand** — same statement.
- **Insider/control-group holding = 906,212,615 shares held by David Vélez Osorno** (2,389,203 Class A personally + 698,914 Class A and 901,624,498 Class B held through Rua California Ltd. + 1,500,000 Class B held by his wife), being 88.3% of Class B and 74.4% of total voting power — `FY25 Form 20-F, Item 7.A Major Shareholders, p.203`. There is no "promoter" concept for a Cayman-incorporated NYSE issuer, so the founder-controlled block is the relevant denominator for the pledge test, per the scan formula.

---

## 1. Disqualifier Check

| # | Disqualifier | Triggered (Y/N) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Auditor qualification or going-concern note (last 3 years) | **N** | Unqualified opinion in all three years. FY2025: "In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements referred to above present fairly, in all material respects... in conformity with International Financial Reporting Standards" — plus a separate clean opinion on internal control over financial reporting under COSO 2013 — `FY25 Form 20-F, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm (KPMG), p.F-3`. Same clean wording FY2023/FY2022 in `FY23 Form 20-F (filed Apr-19-2024), Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm`. Vendor cross-check agrees: Opinion = "Unqualified" for FY2020 through FY2025 — `CIQ Auditors export (NU), data as of Aug-2026 — vendor export`. **No going-concern paragraph anywhere:** the only going-concern wording in the FY25 filing is the routine positive IAS 1 assertion that the statements "were prepared based on the assumption of the Group continuing as a going concern" (`FY25 Form 20-F, Note 2 Basis of preparation`); "substantial doubt" does not appear. The two critical audit matters are both about the expected-credit-loss estimate (US$5,021,996k of ECL on cards and loans at 31-Dec-2025) — a modelling-judgment CAM, not a qualification, and the auditor states it "does not alter in any way our opinion". The 30-Jun-2026 interim statements carry a clean KPMG review conclusion: "nothing has come to our attention that causes us to believe that the accompanying interim condensed consolidated financial statements as at June 30, 2026, are not prepared, in all material respects, in accordance with IAS 34" — `Q2 FY26 Interim Report (14 Aug 2026), Independent Auditors' Report on Review, dated 13 Aug 2026`. |
| 2 | >50% promoter / insider shares pledged | **N** | **Computed: 0% of the control group's own holding pledged, vs the >50% threshold.** No pledge, encumbrance, or margin borrowing against shares by the founder, any director, or any executive officer is disclosed anywhere in the FY2025 filing. The Item 7.A beneficial-ownership table states the company believes "each shareholder identified in the table below possesses sole voting and investment power over all the Class A ordinary shares or Class B ordinary shares shown as beneficially owned" with no pledge footnote — `FY25 Form 20-F, Item 7.A Major Shareholders, p.203`. Every "pledge" hit in the filing is something else: securities the *Group* pledges as trading collateral (US$297,274k margin on the stock exchange, US$899,809k pledged against the company's own margin loan facility — `FY25 Form 20-F, Notes 12 and 24`), or policy language. The insider-trading policy permits employee pledging but bars it in the 15 days before earnings and discloses no actual pledged position — `FY25 Form 20-F, Exhibit 11.1 Insider Trading Policy, "Pledging or margin accounts for Nubankers"`. Nothing pledged is disclosed, so the numerator is zero on the disclosed record. |
| 3 | Related-party transactions >25% of revenue or expenses | **N** | **Computed, each side against its own base: RP sales ÷ revenue ≈ 0.0%; RP purchases/expenses ÷ total expenses = 0.8% — both far below the >25% threshold.** The RPT note is short because all group entities are consolidated and intra-group flows are eliminated: "all entities within the Group are consolidated... Therefore, related party balances and transactions... are eliminated" — `FY25 Form 20-F, Note 28 Related parties, p.F-77`. The only outside related-party item is a liability of **US$926 thousand** at 31-Dec-2025 (US$1,795k at 31-Dec-2024), a cash incentive received under a commercial arrangement with a company whose CEO sits on Nu's board — 0.006% of revenue. The only other IAS 24 line is key-management compensation of **US$91,269 thousand** for FY2025 (FY2024 US$96,007k; FY2023 US$60,117k) — 91,269 ÷ 12,032,723 = **0.76%** of total expenses (0.77% on the net US$11,902,633k base). No related-party revenue is disclosed at all; credit cards and loans issued to directors, officers and key employees are stated to be "on similar terms as those offered to unrelated third parties" and are not quantified separately, which caps any unquantified residual well below materiality given the sizes above. Confirmed unchanged at the latest balance-sheet date: the same note shows US$265k at 30-Jun-2026 — `Q2 FY26 Interim Report (14 Aug 2026), Note 28 Related Parties`. Supply-chain cross-check: `CIQ Suppliers/Customers export (NU), retrieved 2026-09-06 — vendor export` reports `intragroup_row_share_pct` = 0.0 across 11 disclosed relationship rows (scope, quoted: "Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)... Include Customers for:Current subsidiaries"), i.e. no disclosed trading with the group's own entities. |
| 4 | Auditor changed twice in last 3 years without disclosed reason | **N** | Zero changes, let alone two. "We have served as the Company's auditor since 2018." — `FY25 Form 20-F, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm (KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda.), São Paulo, April 8, 2026, p.F-6`. The vendor auditor history shows the same firm for FY2020, FY2021, FY2022, FY2023, FY2024 and FY2025 — `CIQ Auditors export (NU), data as of Aug-2026 — vendor export`. KPMG also signed the 13 Aug 2026 interim review. |
| 5 | Material restatement (>5% of revenue or net income) in last 2 years | **N** | **Computed: restatement amount = US$0, so \|restatement\| ÷ revenue = 0.0% (lead test) and \|restatement\| ÷ \|net income\| = 0.0%, both below the >5% threshold.** Two independent statements in the current filing: the cover-page box "whether the financial statements of the registrant included in the filing reflect the correction of an error to previously issued financial statements" is **unchecked (☐)**, as is the follow-on incentive-compensation recovery box — `FY25 Form 20-F, cover page`. And Item 6.F: "We have not been required to prepare an accounting restatement at any time during or after our last completed fiscal year and no recovery of awarded compensation is required pursuant to our compensation clawback policy" — `FY25 Form 20-F, Item 6.F Disclosure of a Registrant's Action to Recover Erroneously Awarded Compensation, p.202`. The FY2025 auditor's report also carries an unqualified opinion on internal control over financial reporting, and no material weakness is reported. The word "restatement" elsewhere in the filing refers to Brazilian monetary-restatement (inflation indexation) of receivables and to the clawback-policy definitions, not to a correction of Nu's own accounts. |
| 6 | Active regulatory enforcement action on financial reporting | **N** | No enforcement or investigation touching financial reporting by the SEC, the Brazilian Central Bank (BCB), the CVM, Mexico's CNBV, or any other regulator is disclosed. The legal-proceedings disclosure names exactly two non-ordinary matters, both consumer-conduct civil class actions and neither an accounting or reporting matter: (i) a September 2023 class action by the Instituto de Defesa Coletiva over chargeback handling after the 123 Milhas collapse, with amounts involved of approximately **R$7.5 million** and "No material risk has been identified for Nubank"; and (ii) a December 2024 Colombian class action against **52 defendant institutions** including Nu Colombia over automation of the GMF financial-movements-tax exemption, total claim COP 9.3 trillion (about US$2.5 billion as of 16 Jan 2026) apportioned across all 52 defendants, risk classified "possible", Nu's proportionate share not quantifiable at this stage — `FY25 Form 20-F, Item 8.A Legal Proceedings, p.207`. Total judicial deposits were US$6.6 million at 31-Dec-2025 (US$5.7m at 31-Dec-2024), substantially a pre-acquisition withholding-tax matter at Nu Investimentos — same section. Regulatory news in the event record runs the other way: Nu Mexico received CNBV authorisation to operate as a bank — `CIQ Key Developments export (NU), data as of Aug-2026 — vendor export, "Regulatory Authority – Compliance"`. **§24 filter-1 integrity check:** no proven fraud or defrauding of customers, suppliers, employees or shareholders by the controller or senior management appears in the pool; no short-seller report, no unproven adverse allegation against management was found either, so there is nothing soft to route on this count. |
| 7 | >40% of revenue from single customer with no long-term contract | **N** | **Computed: largest single customer <10% of total revenue, vs the >40% threshold.** Stated directly by the company: "The Group had no single customer that represented 10% or more of the Group's revenues in the years ended December 31, 2025, 2024 and 2023." — `FY25 Form 20-F, Note 34 Segment information / Information about major customers`. This is a retail bank whose revenue comes from tens of millions of individual customers, so the disclosed ceiling is a real ceiling, not an absence. The one disclosed institutional customer relationship in the vendor graph (Circle Internet Group, Inc. (NYSE:CRCL), disclosed by the *counterparty* in its own 2025 Form S-1, with Nu Pagamentos S.A. as supplier) is unsized — `CIQ Customers export (NU), retrieved 2026-09-06 — vendor export`. The graph proves the relationship exists; it never sizes one, and the 20-F's <10% statement covers it. |
| 8 | Negative operating cash flow in 3 of last 4 years (excl. growth-stage) | **N** | **Computed: 0 of the last 4 fiscal years had negative operating cash flow, vs the 3-of-4 threshold.** Cash flows generated from operating activities, as filed: FY2022 **+US$755.6m**, FY2023 **+US$1,266.2m**, FY2024 **+US$2,399.0m**, FY2025 **+US$3,500.5m** — `FY23 Form 20-F (filed Apr-19-2024), Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows` (FY2022 and FY2023) and `FY25 Form 20-F, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.F-14` (FY2024 and FY2025). Positive and rising every year; no growth-stage exemption is needed. **Reconciliation to the pinned vendor read (required):** `ciq_facts.json` reports `ltm_ocf_m` = **−10,304.8** (source `CIQ Financials→Cash Flow 'Cash from Ops.' [LTM 12 months Jun-30-2026]`) and the same vendor sheet shows negative operating cash flow in every year FY2021–FY2025. That gap is a **classification difference, not a misread of either document**: Capital IQ applies its Bank template, which moves "Net Incr. (Decr.) in Deposit Accounts" — **+US$12,861.3m in FY2025** — out of operating and into financing, while the company's IFRS statement includes the identical US$12,861,282k deposit inflow inside operating activities. Same underlying number, two shelves. Per CLAUDE.md §4 the audited filing governs the disqualifier test, and the vendor figure is not treated as a distress signal on its own. |

---

## 2. Triggered Disqualifiers — Detail

No disqualifier triggered.

Two observations recorded here so they are not lost, neither of which is a disqualifier and neither of which changes any row above:

- **CFO succession, effective 13 July 2026.** Rob Livingston (previously CFO for North America at Visa) succeeded Guilherme Lago, who had been CFO for five years. Lago supported the transition to 31 August 2026 and remains a special advisor to the management team and to the audit and risk committee — `CIQ Key Developments export (NU), data as of Aug-2026 — vendor export`. A CFO departure is a standing §13 trigger, but the disclosed facts here are a planned, pre-announced succession with the outgoing CFO retained and advising the audit committee, and it coincides with no restatement, no auditor change, and no qualified opinion. Routed to the management-governance module for its own read; not a disqualifier.
- **Open-market insider selling.** `ciq_facts.json` reports open-market insider activity of net −592,707 shares (0 buys / 4 sells) in the trailing twelve months to 2026-08-18, while all-types insider activity is net +4,015,012 (14 acquisitions / 11 disposals) — `CIQ Public Ownership→Insider Trading export (NU), data as of Aug-2026 — vendor export`. Against David Vélez's 906.2m-share block this is immaterial in size, and disqualifier #6 covers enforcement, not routine sales. Routed to `capital-allocation-governance`; not a disqualifier.

---

- **Any disqualifier triggered:** **N**
- **If Y, names:** n/a — none of the eight triggered.
- **Action:** If Y, the synthesizer will lock the final verdict at "Low-quality business — avoid deeper work" regardless of other scores. **No lock is applied here.** This scan clears NU on all eight hard tests only; it makes no statement about business quality, moat, unit economics, or valuation, which are other agents' scope.

*(No `RF-DISQ-001` tag is emitted — see Section 4.)*
